#### Week 6 · Instructor notes (from Version 5 Week 5)

- **Competencies:** 4; 8 (recording).
- **Recording plan:** Version 5 Lecture 5.1 (the interview as listening; recording technique, the slate, room tone and shooting reference), plus a segment on the **video consent**: framing, reading the script, what to do if a narrator hesitates (stop, ask, do not persuade), saving and storing the file, and never uploading it. Demonstration: Gemini Live rehearsal, with typed role-play as the fallback; recording the video consent and slate; room tone; shooting ten reference photographs or short b-roll clips; laying out a journal page by hand or from photographs. **Studio Session** (30 minutes, live, recorded, required to watch): interview clinic, including a live practice of the consent script.
- **RSI cadence:** Monday 22 February at noon: announcement. **S5 feedback Tuesday 23 February at noon** opens the interview window (through Wednesday 3 March). Friday check-in to anyone without an interview day. S6 is due Thursday 4 March.
- **Seat time:** Direct 2.5 (lecture 0.5, demo 0.5, Studio Session 0.5, critique for P3 and P4 1.0). Outside 5.5 (see 4.2).
- **Common pitfalls:** starting the interview before the Consent Affirmation is on file; skipping the video consent; uploading the recording to AI before the Signed Agreement (not allowed); photographing the narrator without asking; AI-generated journal backgrounds (no longer allowed).

**[Title box] Week 6 · Gathering: The Interview**

**Objectives** · Module 6 objectives. By the end of this module, you will be able to:
- 6.1 Apply scene-seeking questions and follow-ups in a rehearsed interview (Course Competency 4)
- 6.2 Record a consented interview with clean, usable audio, and gather visual reference from the story’s places (Course Competency 4)
- 6.3 Develop your connection to the story in a visual field journal (Course Competency 4)

**Overview** · The interview is your primary source. It opens with the **video-recorded consent**: your narrator reads a short statement on camera agreeing to be recorded and confirming they understand how AI tools will be used. Then you slate, interview, record room tone, and gather the light, objects and places that will become frames later. Your interview window opens Tuesday, February 23, at noon and runs through Wednesday, March 3. This week has two deadlines: P3 on Thursday, February 25, and P4 on Sunday, February 28, both at noon. S6, the interview itself, is due Thursday, March 4.

**Watch** · Lecture 6.1, The Interview as Listening, and the video consent · Demonstration · Studio Session: interview clinic.

**Read** · StudioBinder, [“Ultimate Guide to Camera Shots”](https://www.studiobinder.com/blog/ultimate-guide-to-camera-shots/), the sections on wide, medium and close-up shots.

**Practice**
- *P3 Interview Rehearsal* (10 points). Measures Module Objective 6.1 (Course Competency 4). Unchanged from Version 5, Week 5: a 10-minute rehearsal with Gemini playing a narrator you describe in two sentences (no private details about your real narrator), using Gemini Live where available; three changes to your guide and the best follow-up, posted; the chat share link in Canvas only. Add one step: rehearse the video consent script aloud once, playing the narrator yourself, and note anything you will explain differently.
- *P4 Visual Field Journal* (10 points). Measures Module Objective 6.3 (Course Competency 4). Version 5, Week 5, with one change: the pages are made from sketches, collage and your own reference photographs only. No AI-generated textures or images. Three pages (person, place, memory), each with a 40 to 60 word sensory caption; the place page uses at least three reference photographs or clips you shot on location.

**Assessment** · *S6 The Interview and Video Consent* is conducted in this window and due Thursday, March 4. Read the S6 assignment page (Week 7) before your interview. Begin scanning and photographing your narrator’s items this week; scanning is not an AI step, so it may start before the Signed Agreement.

**Critique / Discussion** · No discussion this week; critique happens in P3 and P4. Use the Course Questions channel and the Studio Session.

#### Week 7 · Instructor notes (from Version 5 Week 6 and the M4 half of Week 7)

- **Competencies:** 10; 4.
- **Recording plan:** Lecture 7.1 (two segments, shortened because the mini taught archiving): from the Practice Archive to a real one (what changes when the archive belongs to a person: consent, privacy, cultural protocols, sensitive information); the Signed Agreement item by item, and how each yes or no limits what you may do. Demonstration (about 30 minutes): scanning with a phone; Premiere Pro speech-to-text on an excerpt after the agreement is signed; redaction; the metadata sheet. Guided lab (30 minutes): building the real archive folder from the template, including the 06_Consent folder and its sharing settings (instructor only).
- **RSI cadence:** Monday 1 March at noon: announcement with the upload rule in the first line. **S6 feedback by Sunday 7 March at noon. S7 feedback by Tuesday 9 March at noon**, confirming the agreement is complete and uploads may begin.
- **Seat time:** Direct 2.5 (lecture 0.5, demo 0.5, guided lab 0.5, critique for S6 0.5, feedback review 0.5). Outside 5.5 (see 4.2).
- **Common pitfalls:** uploading before the agreement; uploading the consent video anywhere; Agreement items left blank (blank is no); posting interview audio to Discord (never; S6’s critique post is reference photographs only). **Narrator cancellations:** a student who tells the instructor by Wednesday 3 March may submit S6 by Monday 8 March at noon with no deduction.

**[Title box] Week 7 · Building the Archive**

**Objectives** · Module 7 objectives. By the end of this module, you will be able to:
- 7.1 Organize a real story archive with a signed agreement, consistent names and folders, accurate metadata and alt text (Course Competency 10)
- 7.2 Correct an AI transcript so that it preserves your narrator’s own words (Course Competency 4)

**Overview** · You built an archive in Week 2 from the Practice Archive. This week you build the real one, and three things are different: the material belongs to a person; your narrator’s **Signed Agreement** decides what you may do with each item; and nothing goes into an AI tool until that agreement is signed with item 4 marked yes. This week has two deadlines: S6 on Thursday, March 4, and S7 on Sunday, March 7, both at noon. S7 has no critique because it contains private material.

**Watch** · Lecture 7.1, From the Practice Archive to a Real One · Demonstration: scanning, speech-to-text after the agreement, redaction, metadata · Guided Lab: the archive folder and consent folder.

**Read** · USC Shoah Foundation, [Dimensions in Testimony overview](https://sfi.usc.edu/dit) and [FAQ](https://sfi.usc.edu/dit/faq), and 10 minutes with [Pinchas Gutter on IWitness](https://iwitness.usc.edu/dit/pinchas) (free account may be required). Notice that every answer is the person’s real recorded words, given with their consent.

**Practice** · No separate practice this week.

**Assessment**

*S6 The Interview and Video Consent* (10 points, Process Work). This assignment measures Module Objective 6.2, which supports Course Competency 4.
1. Confirm the Consent Affirmation is on file before you begin.
2. Record the **video consent** first: your slate, then your narrator reading the consent script on camera (or you reading each sentence and your narrator answering “Yes, I agree”). A guardian appears too for a narrator under 18. If your narrator hesitates or says no to anything, stop and ask what they would like to change. Save the file as `YYYY-MM-DD_consent_lastname.mp4` in 06_Consent and back it up the same day. Never upload it to any AI tool or to Discord.
3. Conduct the interview (20 to 30 minutes) using your guide.
4. Record 30 seconds of room tone, and one minute of ambient sound from a place in the story if you can get there.
5. Close by asking, “Is there anything you said that you would rather I not use?” Note the answer.
6. Back up the recording in two places the same day.
7. Within 24 hours, write a shoot log of about 100 words using the template: setting, light, sound, anything off the record, concerns, and the reference shots you captured.
8. Give your narrator the Signed Agreement to read; it may be signed now or within the week.
9. Post three reference photographs from the interview location (no people unless they agreed to class sharing) in Discord #s6-reference by Wednesday at noon. Comment on at least three classmates’ photographs by Thursday at noon, with one comment on light or framing.
10. Add a row to your AI-use log and submit in Canvas only: the consent video, the interview recording, room tone, ambient sound, the shoot log, the Agreement status and your critique record.

If your narrator has to reschedule, tell the instructor by Wednesday, March 3; you may then submit S6 by Monday, March 8, at noon with no deduction.

*S7 Signed Agreement, Transcript and Archive Inventory* (10 points, Process Work; no critique). This assignment measures Module Objective 7.1, which supports Course Competency 10.
1. Meet with your narrator (in person, by phone or by video call) to go through the **Signed Agreement** item by item. Answer questions; do not encourage any particular answer. Collect the signature (and the guardian’s, or the family member’s or estate holder’s for Part D, where it applies). Self-interview: complete it for yourself.
2. Read the signed agreement and list what it allows and restricts. Until item 4 is yes, nothing from the interview goes into any AI tool. If item 4 is no, follow the no-AI path on the AI in This Course page.
3. After the agreement is signed, create a transcript first draft with Premiere Pro speech-to-text or Gemini Notebook (or by hand). Cut any passages about immigration or legal status, legal cases or health details, anything your narrator asked you not to use, and anything item 11 restricts, before any further AI use.
4. Choose the 10 minutes you are most likely to use and correct that excerpt fully against the audio, keeping your narrator’s exact words. Label the rest “machine draft, uncorrected.”
5. Build your archive folder from the template (01_Interview, 02_Images, 03_Documents, 04_Audio, 05_Journal, 06_Consent, 07_Production). Share 06_Consent with the instructor only.
6. Add at least 10 items, named `YYYY-MM-DD_subject_description` (use “ca1975” or “undated” when the date is unknown). Include one object photographed as a still life (window light, plain background, deliberate framing). Your P4 pages and reference photographs count as items.
7. Describe every item in the metadata sheet: title, date (marked as a guess where it is one), creator, description, source, and Agreement status (which items cover it). Gemini may draft descriptions only for items the agreement clears for AI; check every name and date.
8. Write alt text for the five images you are most likely to use.
9. Add a row to your AI-use log and submit in Canvas: the signed agreement, the corrected excerpt, the folder link (instructor only) and the metadata sheet.

**Critique / Discussion** · No discussion this week. The Teller Exchange is in Week 8.

#### Week 8 · Instructor notes (from the Teller half of Version 5 Weeks 7 and 8)

- **Competencies:** 10; 3; 5 (through D5).
- **Recording plan:** Lecture 8.1 (three segments): what a language model does, in plain terms (recap from the mini); grounding, and why grounded tools still invent where an archive has gaps; Fredbot versus a Gemini Notebook, now with the student’s real narrator in mind; the voice choice and the Agreement items it needs (item 5 or 6; Part D). Guided lab (three segments): moving from the practice Teller to the real one; guardrails tied to logged problems; the 20-question test. **Studio Session** (one hour, live, recorded, required to watch): Teller troubleshooting, ending with a short introduction to style frames (one example per form) for Week 9.
- **RSI cadence:** Monday 8 March at noon: announcement naming the shifted Saturday deadline and that nothing is due over spring break. **S8 feedback by Tuesday 23 March at noon** (spring break not counted). Mid-semester check-in: a short individual note on each student’s progress. Ask three held-out questions of each Teller during grading.
- **Seat time:** Direct 3.0. Outside 5.0 (see 4.2).
- **Common pitfalls:** first-person voice without item 6 (or Part D); Tellers loaded with material item 11 restricts; posting a Teller about someone who has died without the opt-in line; expecting work over spring break (nothing is due; style frames are due after the break, at the Week 9 deadline).

**[Title box] Week 8 · The Teller**

**Objectives** · Module 8 objectives. By the end of this module, you will be able to:
- 8.1 Build a Teller grounded in your real archive, with written instructions and guardrails, and test and correct it (Course Competency 10)
- 8.2 Explain how a grounded AI tool answers from sources and why it can still invent (Course Competency 3)
- 8.3 Present your Teller to peers for review and question theirs (Course Competency 5)

**Overview** · You built a practice Teller in Week 2. Now you build the real one from your narrator’s cleared material. Your Teller answers only from the sources you load, with citations, and follows the instructions you write; it is never public, and it is shown only by screen recording. Choose its voice: the **archivist voice** (third person, quoting the archive; Agreement item 5) or the **first-person voice** (Agreement item 6 for a living narrator, or Part D for someone who has died). Either voice is graded the same. This week has two deadlines: S8 on Thursday, March 11, and D5 on **Saturday**, March 13, both at noon. D5 is a day early so that nothing is due or closing over spring break (March 15 to 21).

*Content note:* this week’s optional reading on chatbots built from people who have died discusses grief and a young person’s death. An alternate reading is listed.

**Watch** · Lecture 8.1, How a Teller Works · Guided Lab: from practice Teller to real Teller · Studio Session: Teller troubleshooting and an introduction to style frames.

**Read** (choose one path; both take about 45 minutes) · Version 5 Week 7, Path A reading or Path B Under the Hood lab, unchanged.

**Practice** · *Mid-semester survey* (ungraded), due with D5. · *Style-frame thumbnails* (not submitted): before the break, sketch small thumbnails for three to five style frames; you will finish them in S9.

**Assessment** · *S8 Teller Build and Test* (10 points, Process Work; no critique, because it contains private material). This assignment measures Module Objective 8.1, which supports Course Competency 10.
1. Create a Gemini Notebook and load at least 12 items the Signed Agreement clears for AI: your corrected transcript excerpt, at least five captioned images with their metadata, your P4 journal pages, and any documents your narrator approved.
2. Write the instruction scaffold in your own words, building on your practice Teller: who the Teller is, whose archive it holds, its voice and the Agreement item that permits it, what it must never do (including every item 11 restriction), and what it says when the archive has no answer.
3. Test the Teller with 20 questions, including at least 5 the archive cannot answer. Rate each answer accurate, partly accurate or invented.
4. Change your instructions or sources to fix at least three problems, then ask those questions again.
5. Complete the half-page Teller Design Form: final instructions; sources loaded; voice and the Agreement item that permits it; three guardrails, each pointing to the logged problem that caused it.
6. Make a 2-minute screen recording of your Teller answering at least four questions (for D5).
7. Share the notebook with the instructor only [verify named-person sharing].
8. Add a row to your AI-use log and submit the share link, design form, test log and screen recording.

**Critique / Discussion** · *D5 Teller Exchange* (10 points). This assignment measures Module Objective 8.3, which supports Course Competency 5.
- *Prompt:* Post your 2-minute screen recording and one sentence about whose archive the Teller holds. If your Teller is about someone who has died, mark the first line “opt-in”; classmates choose whether to view it. If your Agreement does not allow class sharing (item 2), post a recording with private details cut, or describe one fix you made with a before-and-after screenshot. In your comments, give three classmates one question each for their Teller; owners run the questions and reply with the answers by Saturday at noon. Compare one answer with what your practice Teller did in Week 2.
- *Your post:* the recording and one sentence, in Discord #d5-teller-exchange.
- *Critique:* questions for at least three classmates’ Tellers by Friday evening, and answers to the questions asked of yours by Saturday at noon.
- *Instructor role:* asks questions of at least five Tellers in the channel; Sunday summary of common fixes.

**Due** · [Date box] S8 · No critique · Due: Thursday, March 11, 2027, at noon · Hard close: Friday, March 12, at noon. · [Date box] D5 and survey · Post: **Friday**, March 12, 2027, at noon, Discord #d5-teller-exchange · Due: **Saturday**, March 13, at noon · Hard close: **Sunday**, March 14, at noon (10% deduction; nothing accepted after). Spring break follows; nothing is due March 15 to 21.

## 8. Verify before teaching

- Google Workspace for Education terms: Gemini and Gemini Notebook do not use school-account data to train models or for human review; current retention after deletion (quoted in 2.4 and 2.7).
- Adobe for Education terms: customer content is not used to train generative AI; which Adobe features process content in the cloud (Premiere Pro speech-to-text, Enhance Speech, Adobe Podcast).
- Gemini Notebook: custom instruction setting and length limit; named-person sharing for student accounts; whether image files can be loaded as sources.
- Gemini Live and Gemini in Docs, Sheets and Gmail for student accounts.
- Every Practice Archive image (3.5): item chosen, rights statement read, credit line written.
- Whether the college or district requires its own media release or recording consent form in place of, or in addition to, the Signed Agreement.
- Records retention: how long the college must keep submitted consent documents and the consent video, and where (Canvas submissions or a separate store).
- The *Artificial* passages used in D2 and the page blocks for Weeks 1 to 4.
- Pixar in a Box lesson names; the Gemini Notebook help page address; GCC Counseling, Dean of Students and Disability Resources links.

## 9. Open questions (most important first)

1. **Legal review of the consent package.** The Signed Agreement is written as a contract and the video consent creates a recording of the narrator’s face. Should district legal or risk management review Sections 2.4 to 2.7 before the first offering, and does the college already have a release form that must be used?
2. **Which term to date.** The faculty review asked for Spring 2027 dates, but the build is planned for a first offering in Spring 2028, and the 24 October standard says course designs carry week numbers only. Keep the Spring 2027 dates as a worked example, re-date for Spring 2028, or strip dates from the design and keep them only in the Canvas package?
3. **Project weights.** Under the standard, Project 1 (the three-week mini on fictional material) and the Final are both 30 points, so each is 17.5% of the grade. Keep them equal, or weight the Final higher?
4. **A narrator who will not be filmed.** Is the hands-and-page alternative in 2.6 (voice on camera, face out of frame) acceptable as video consent?
5. **The Practice Archive voice.** Who reads Ada Ruiz’s lines: you, or a volunteer reader who signs the Agreement? (No synthetic voice either way.)
6. **Generative images in Weeks 10 to 15.** Remove the remaining image-generation uses listed in 7.4 to match “Gemini writes; it does not design,” or keep any (for example, Generative Expand to fill a 16:9 frame, labeled)?
7. **Weights.** The standard’s 40 / 35 / 25 replaces the Version 5 weights you confirmed on 23 October (Practice 10, Discussions 15, Milestones 20, Project 1 20, Final 35). Confirm.
8. **Studio Sessions** move from Weeks 5 and 7 to Weeks 6 and 8. Confirm, and set days and times.
